### CHORE-115 — Android asset protocol answers Range requests with a non-zero start wrongly; asset videos stall after about 1 MB

**Status (2026-10-24, lane A records batch 6): fixed by wry 0.57.1 in builds made from this checkout, MEASURED on phone 2 (`c115\result.md` in `%TEMP%\risu-b715\`); pending the next MVP build.** `cargo update -p wry --precise 0.57.1` moved only wry (0.57.0 to 0.57.1, which contains PR #1865). On phone 2 (release build of `f0e397aae` with wry 0.57.1, test key), ranges with a non-zero start return exact 206 responses with correct `Content-Length`, and the asset files reassemble to their manifest sha256. Caveats: a range starting exactly at the file's size still fails rather than returning 416; the video check did not run. The lock is local only (`src-tauri/Cargo.lock` is gitignored), so the maintainer's next sign-release build from this checkout picks it up. The installed MVP (`f0e397aae`) still has wry 0.57.0.

**Status (2026-10-22): post-MVP (MC-345).** The maintainer: "chore-115 can be optional, as animated asset is rare in my save data." Parked until after the A5 checkpoint; the wry release check below still applies before any Android release.

**Status (2026-10-21) (superseded by the status above): open; PR #1865 is merged in wry (`b6bef412`, 2026-10-08) but no release carries it yet: the latest wry release is v0.57.0 (2026-09-08), 7 commits before the merge, and `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` resolves wry 0.57.0 and tauri 2.12.1 (checked with `gh` and the lock, 2026-10-21). Still waiting for a wry release containing the fix (`MC-230`); check before the Android release.** Acceptance (the Orchestrator's proposal, not a maintainer decision): the Stage C C2 fetches return the right length and the mp4 plays past 1 MB on the Note 9. It predates CHORE-109: `getFileSrc` at `f42cdad9` already returned `urlFor` or `convertFileSrc` on Tauri (INFERRED from source; the old build was not run).

- **Observed (Stage C, Note 9, Android 10, WebView 153, a debug build of `9ec38568`, single runs; Report 61 section 8):** `bytes=0-15` and `bytes=0-` give a correct 206 (`0-` capped at 1,024,000 B); any Range with a start above 0 has a `Content-Length` short by exactly the start, or fails with `ERR_FAILED`; an mp4 stops after about 1 MB with `MEDIA_ERR_NETWORK` (logcat `PIPELINE_ERROR_READ`).
- **Cause (ledger row 1332; TRACED in source, no device run):** tauri 2.11.5 `asset.rs` returns a correct ranged 206; wry 0.55.1 wraps the body in a `ByteArrayInputStream` and adds no Range logic; Chromium's Android WebView stream loader reads the request's Range and skips the start again on that already-ranged body, so `Content-Length` is N minus the start, and a start at or past N fails. The Stage C C2 data points match that arithmetic (the investigator counts 12). The Chromium source read was `main`, not the WebView 153 tag (INFERRED that 153 matches). Ranges past 2 GiB also fail (Chromium reads `available()` as an Int32). Upstream: wry issue #1864 and PR #1865 (approved and unmerged at filing; merged 2026-10-08, unreleased as of 2026-10-21; the Orchestrator confirmed both with `gh`). Not fixed in wry 0.57.0 or tauri 2.12.1. Desktop WebView2 is INFERRED unaffected (no run); Linux WebKitGTK has a separate video issue (tauri#3725).
- **Options considered (investigator, row 1332):** **A** wait for upstream (no files; timing unknown; the lock floats, so a fixed wry arrives by itself once tauri-runtime-wry allows it, INFERRED) is the one the maintainer chose (`MC-230`). **B** a `[patch.crates-io]` wry git fork with PR #1865's Kotlin hunk (about 100 lines; S to M; must match the wry version the floating build resolves; untested until a first build). **E** a Blob URL for Android video as a stopgap (about 3 times the file size in transient memory; needs a size cap and a fallback message). **C** a repo-side Kotlin change, not feasible (the file is generated from wry's template; INFERRED, not compiled). **D** our own protocol handler, which needs O(start) or O(file) memory (OOM reported in tauri#12019), rejected except under a small cap. **F** a local 127.0.0.1 HTTP server, correct with flat memory, but a new dependency, a re-implemented scope and a lifecycle (M to L). None touches saves, assets on disk or upstream compatibility (`MC-175`). Unknowns: WebView 153 against Chromium `main`; PR #1865 on our pipeline (needs one patched build and a C2 rerun).
- Android video stays broken until a wry release with the fix arrives.

Final-snapshot checks (Orchestrator, 2026-10-08): `pnpm test` (CI=true) 569 files, 11,582 passed, 6 skipped; `pnpm check` 0 errors and 0 warnings; `pnpm build` passes from a clean scratch copy that excludes `Agents/` (an in-place build fails only on the untracked handoff notes' path fragment under Tailwind's source scan, an environment caveat that ends when that evidence is archived or ignored). The restore-repair notice is `MC-248`. Queued after CHORE-116 by `MC-234` ("queue it after chore 116"); the number is assigned by the order in which the hold note below lists the two queued tickets. Type: bug, persistence. Maintainer decisions D1 to D8: `MC-247` (D4 superseded by D5). The maintainer kept the CHORE-117 and CHORE-118 ids (`MC-245` 6).

- **Stage split (D7, `MC-247`; stage contents from the Orchestrator handoff section 3, not maintainer decisions):**
  - **Stage 1a:** Gate 1 passed (plan-1a round 1 `[REJECT]`, round 2 `[EDITORIAL]`, E6 applied and verified). Accepted plan: `plan-1a.md` plus `plan-1a-r2.md` in `Agents/Handoff-2026-10-08/chore117/`, which also lists non-blocking N2 to N5 and optional O1 for the implementation brief. Implemented, Gate 2 approved and committed as `0d4efeb1` (see the status above). Scope per the handoff: the encoder never writes unloadable bytes and reports what it left out; the save loop alone owns a resumable pause; a last-line header gate in the block store; non-character entries dropped and missing ids filled; a tree planner before every boot install and on restores and exports (a stub's id taken from its unit); plugin setters reject newly bad ids; boot hardening for `personas` and folders; export never refuses. Disclosed interim gaps, closed by 1b: a saved character renamed to a falsy id gets a fresh id and its links dangle (the reviewer confirmed the same outcome as today; only an absent or `undefined` id is worse today, since that file does not load); a never-saved character with a bad id pauses instead of taking a fresh id; a paused character's export copy gets a fresh id.
  - **Stage 1a disclosed residuals (remaining work):** a non-array container read from disk keeps today's `checkNewFormat` behaviour; cold-unit `saying` is not remapped after a re-id; `manualCleanup` does not refuse during a pause; S16 (a peer-tab save while paused) is not driven; S15 1000-character byte-identity is not tested at scale; the M2 red-at-base used a staged base (M1 fixes present), not HEAD. Behaviour changes: `checkCharOrder` prunes non-string, non-folder entries when nothing is held; unusable-id marks are dropped from the tracker snapshot; a whole-list `setDatabase` checks per-id counts, so held ids may move (documented in `plugins.md`). The 9 new locale keys are not native-reviewed (the `de` plural constructions are flagged).
  - **Stage 1b:** not planned (a page-lifetime record of saved objects and last-saved keys; never-saved re-id; falsy-rename pause; export with the last-saved id; scope per the handoff).
  - **Stage 2:** not planned (values `JSON.stringify` cannot serialize: cycles, BigInt, a throwing `toJSON`; today they make the save loop retry forever).
  - The handoff suggests filing 1a, 1b and 2 as separate Roadmap tickets; that is a maintainer choice and is not done here, so they stay stages of CHORE-117.
- **"reading length" backups (corrects the text below):** this was investigated, not left open. The investigation packet (`chore117/facts.md`, ledger row 1442) found that the two oldest Note 9 backups' "reading length" error matches V8's message for `decodeRisuSave(undefined)` (a caller with no bytes; message confirmed by execution, device cause inferred; the device files were not examined), and that truncation of block-format v1 at 2,435 cut points never produces it (legacy msgpack/fflate/gzip formats not exercised; UNCERTAIN). It is not a save-time issue; no code. Source: `Agents/Handoff-2026-10-08/chore117/facts.md` lines 95-101. The filing-time sentence "the two oldest backups fail with 'reading length' (not investigated)" below is kept as the historical filing text.

- **Observed (ledger row 1365, `investigator`, TRACED):** a save written on the Note 9 would not load on the next start. The damaged block was a `characters` entry that is the bare number 5 (the return value of `createNewGroup`, likely pushed by a test agent). The encoder keys `String(undefined)` and writes an empty header name with no check; the loader's `checkSingleBlock` rejects it as wrong-name. The save and the numbered backup both succeeded, then the next start refused to load. The boot repair skips non-objects. No ordinary UI route was found; plugin writes of non-objects were not examined. Backup 17913433458 decodes strictly (5 characters); the two oldest backups fail with "reading length" (not investigated).
- **Direction (non-normative):** validate at the encoder or the commit so that a committed generation always loads, with a red-before-green test through `loadBlockProfile`. It touches persistence, so it goes through a plan gate and `opus-reviewer`. Look at the "reading length" backups too (see the correction above).

### Chat-screen render findings (investigation 2026-10-09): CHORE-154 to CHORE-157

**Evidence and confidence.** Source: investigator packet `chatperf\packet.md` (snapshot `8acadfe0`) and its independent verification `chatperf\verify.md` (deep-investigator, snapshot `7921b287`; verdict "HOLDS WITH CORRECTIONS"; mechanisms confirmed). Where the two differ the verification is used. The micro-benchmarks are on the i9-13900KF (best-case hardware) with synthetic data; **nothing was measured on a phone or Pi**, and the packet adopts no scale factor (CHORE-45 estimates 10 to 15 times slower). Premises the investigation refuted or narrowed: `addMetadataToElement` never runs (`aiWatermarkingLawApplies()` returns `false`); only the streaming message is re-parsed per chunk (the window-wide cost is the hash loop and a forced layout); global custom CSS is one event-driven `<style>`. **Queue position:** after the windowing of other lists (`MC-256` 4). **Gate:** each ticket needs a Gate 1 plan and the post-implementation review; rendered output must stay identical except where `MC-257` decides a change (CHORE-156). The equivalence-risk ratings are the packet's, not a maintainer rule.

### CHORE-156 — Decided chat-render work items (streaming default, Lua engine reuse, superseded parses)

**Status (2026-10-27, lane A records batch 17): item 1 is dropped, superseded by the UI design session's DD-071 "Slice 1 append-only" (`MC-773`; cross-session reference, not verified here); items 2 and 3 remain.** Lane A's answer: "Drop item 1 (Recommended)". `MC-257` carries the supersession note for item 1.

**Status (2026-10-09): decided (`MC-257`); queued after the windowing items; not planned.** These three were behaviour questions in the investigation; the maintainer answered them, so they are work items. Each needs a Gate 1 plan.

1. **Default streaming display mode becomes "balanced"** (`MC-257` 1; resolves the open question of `MC-051`). Post-processing then runs at most about every 0.125 s while streaming instead of remounting on every chunk. The default is `?? 'off'` in `Chats.svelte` and the database default (`database.svelte.ts:699`). Before the switch: verify behaviour on heavy-script cards (the setting is labelled experimental). **Plan question for Gate 1, not decided:** whether existing profiles with no stored value change.
2. **One Lua engine per script** (`MC-257` 2): cache the engine by mode plus code instead of mode only (`scriptings.ts:1364-1393`; recreate test at `:210-217`; the loop over every `triggerlua` at `:1595-1612`). Behaviour change: multi-script setups keep Lua globals between calls, as single-script setups already do; a card that relied on the reset (unlikely) differs from upstream. Measured on the i9 in Node with an empty-ish Lua: engine create plus 150 API functions plus a small `doString` 1.83 ms against 0.03 ms for a reused call; real card Lua is unmeasured. How many real cards ship two or more `triggerlua` triggers is unknown (not to be probed on community services).
3. **Superseded streaming parses finish but their output is dropped** (`MC-257` 3): stale parses still run, including Lua side effects such as `setChatVar`; their result is not put on screen. This saves the extra DOM work, not the Lua time. No abort check exists today (`ChatBody.svelte:72-182`; the Lua call queues on `mutex.runExclusive`, `scriptings.ts:203`). The investigation's open measurement still applies: on a mid-range phone and a Pi-class device with the default mode, the share of each chunk spent in remount, hash loop, forced layout and editoutput scripts, and whether the abandoned-parse backlog grows.

### CHORE-157 — Chat render small items: `ScriptingEditDisplayIds` never cleared; `getClosestMatch` overwrites multi-variant assets

**Status (2026-10-09): open; queued after the windowing items.** Type: bugs, small.

### CHORE-161 — Android boot baseline follow-ups: timers starved during `encoder.init`, and main-thread work after the boot marker

**Status (2026-10-09): open; performance follow-ups, not planned.** Type: performance, Android. Source: investigator report `baseline.md` (perf-analyzer, Note 9, instrumented release build, unarchived synthetic tiers) as corrected by `baseline-verify.md` (verdict ACCEPT WITH CORRECTIONS), session scratch; ledger rows 1515 and 1526. Medians reproduced by the verifier; the corrections below are the verifier's.

- **Timers starved for about 5.9 s during `encoder.init`.** Heartbeat gaps `HBLAG gap=5859` (tier B, run 3) and 5988 (tier C, run 3), with `encoder.init` at 5,785 ms (B) and 5,810 ms (C) at about 166 MB decoded, independent of character count (500 or 1000), about 33 ms/MB. The baseline called this "a single main-thread block"; the verifier corrected that: `encoder.init` yields to the event loop once at least 8 ms have passed, so the candidate "yield between characters" is already in `HEAD`, and the long-task observer reports no 5 s task. What is supported is that timer macrotasks did not run for 5.9 s; the cause is the verifier's inference (the yield continuations run ahead of timers), untested. Whether touch and paint were served during that time was not measured, so the "first interactive" figures (about 12.2 s for tier B, 14.1 s for tier C, from process start) may be too late; an input-latency probe would settle it.
- **The main thread is not free after `bootSaveSequence:end`.** After the marker the logs show long tasks: tier B 21 to 26 long tasks (1.8 to 2.4 s) until the capture ended at about 15.9 s; tier C run 3: 190 long tasks (10.8 s), run 5: 86 long tasks (18.1 s, about 90 percent busy per 2 s bucket from 14 to 34 s), capture ending near 34.6 s. The baseline's note that this "disappeared in later boots" is false for tier C (present in all six runs). The cause is unknown (the baseline guessed avatar thumbnail work; not confirmed).
- **Scope of these numbers:** unarchived profiles, one device, warm page cache, instrumented release build (about 70 extra IPC calls per boot for the markers; overhead not isolated). With the archive pass on, boots of a 1000-character tier C profile reached home in 1.9 s on the Note 9 (device pass 1, a different measurement method); whether these two costs matter for archived profiles was not measured.

### CHORE-162 — Android boot baseline follow-up: per-call IPC cost of block reads (batching)

**Status (2026-10-09): open; performance follow-up, not planned.** Type: performance, Android. Source: `baseline.md` and `baseline-verify.md` as in CHORE-161.

- **Observed:** boot reads one block per `read_range` invoke. The block-read phase fit `about 2.6 ms x calls + 11.4 ms x MB` over four tiers (a descriptive fit of four points, largest miss 14 percent); the Rust handler is only 25 to 30 percent of the phase's wall time; raising the read concurrency from 8 to 32 changed the phase by about minus 5 percent (n=5, a different APK).
- **Mechanism, corrected by the verifier:** the baseline attributed the time to `RustWebViewClient`; the verifier read that on Android, tauri 2.12.1 sends commands through `window.ipc.postMessage` and returns the bytes through a UI-thread `webview.eval` plus a JS `fetch` of the channel data, so each read has at least four stages beyond the file read, and the per-call term cannot be attributed to one of them from these data.
- **Candidate (the baseline's, not approved):** one batched read command, or a packed key for several characters, instead of one invoke per block (it removes the per-call stages; the per-byte term stays). Not planned or gated.

A recreation caused by a configuration change that is not declared in the manifest was not run on F.

### CHORE-166 — Timers that are still late while the app is hidden after Stage 3b

**Status (2026-10-27, lane A records batch 16; `MC-693`, `MC-694`): the stage 2C wake fix is on `main` as `034723414`; not COMPLETE.** Source: lane A's log lines 886 to 926; the scratch paths are under `%TEMP%\risu-b715\c166\s2c-gap\`. Lane A traced the batch 15 failure (ledger rows 4511 to 4522). Step 2 on phone 2 (a scratch build with measurement-only instrumentation) committed 5 of 5 large and 2 of 2 small replies while hidden, and every one of 8 freezes landed at the idle `port.wait(500)` wait; the earlier 2a loss was not reproduced on that source (MEASURED on the scratch build; the 2a mechanism stays INFERRED). The fix, planned through two Gate 1 rounds ([REJECT], then [APPROVE WITH AMENDMENTS]) and a Gate 2 [APPROVE], lets a save request wake the store2 loop out of its back-off on a frozen hidden Android page and skips the network yield. Phone 2 A/B with scratch fault hooks (`phone-ab\result.md`; lane A's reading, no independent check): H1 (120 s back-off, then Home) 3 of 3 stalled before the fix and 3 of 3 committed after; H2 (freeze at the network-slot yield) 3 of 3 fired trials stalled before and 3 of 3 fired trials committed after (the hook fired in 3 of 4 trials before and 3 of 8 after; trials where it did not fire are not counted). A back-off begun visible was not measured on the fixed build. Full check over main `523ca8ac4`: the first run had 7 failures in 5 files (all pass alone), the second 25,140 passed and 0 failed; check 0, build and dist passed. Residuals the commit message lists under "Not covered" (`final\commit-msg.txt`): (1) a Raw read in flight at the freeze, including stub loads under the lock; (2) other `dbWriteLock` holders (local backup, internal backup, import, restore); (3) a stale race waiter after `Promise.race` on a deferral can absorb a mid-pass request without setting the latch; (4) a yield begun while visible that spans the freeze (inferred negligible); (5) `foldBack(null)` returns before `requestPass` when a failure precedes the snapshot, so `isDue()` is not true during every back-off; (6) the earlier 2a loss is not proven closed by this fix. The independent verifier for the step 1 trace and the step 2 phone result is TODO(evidence).

**Status (2026-10-27, lane A records batch 15; `MC-420` to `MC-427`, `MC-693`): stages 1, 2A, 2B and 2C are on `main`; not COMPLETE.** Source: lane A's log for 2026-10-25 to 2026-10-27; the scratch paths are under `%TEMP%\risu-b715\c166\`.
- **Stage 1 (`a24e94e3a`, committed and pushed on "Commit it now as stage 1", `MC-422`):** a finished reply is saved at once while hidden. Its phone check A6 on phone 2 failed (reply absent after a force-stop 2.9 s after the end); the diagnostics showed that a non-streamed response that lands after about 60 s hidden is not processed, and streamed replies freeze at 55 to 60 s. The maintainer reopened the keep-timers "No" because that "no" rested on an analysis that claimed timers were unreliable (`MC-422`).
- **Stage 2A (`85378dc9e`; hold until the hidden save commits):** the keep-alive holds the save while the app is hidden, with no time limit. Becoming visible does not release it; it is released when the save commits or stops, or after about 2 minutes of continuous visibility (`HIDDEN_REPLY_SAVE_VISIBLE_BOUND_MS = 120000`). The maintainer chose "Accept 2-min bound" on 2026-10-27, which amends the 2026-10-25 accepted limit "30-min backstop applies only on next open" (`MC-693`). Notice text: "Reply ready. Open the app to finish saving." (`MC-423`). Accepted limit: swiping the app from recents kills a pending save (phone proof: 68 of 88 characters kept). The silent-sound option was rejected (`MC-424`). Gate 2 [APPROVE].
- **Stage 2B (`de3318b7b`; approach approved in `MC-425`):** a `read_range_text` String command while hidden, and the encoder yield made immediate while hidden. Phone proof: S2-B device PASS on phone 2 (`s2b-device\result.md`); E5 PASS on phone 1 (`s2b-e5\result.md`).
- **Stage 2C (`4c6f755b7`):** Android non-streamed chat requests go through `fetchNative` on the event route, with no time limit (`MC-425`, `MC-426`); the timeout rename applies to streaming too, as the whole request (`MC-427`). The maintainer confirmed on 2026-10-27 that they approved this commit; the exact words were not logged. **Phone 2 device proof (`s2c-device\result.md`, run 2026-10-26 on APK `0197859A…ABB33C`; MIXED, MEASURED, ledger row 4506):** d90 small (100 B), d300 and s90 (streaming regression) passed, n=1 each, and the visible 2 KB sanity check passed (display and save, byte-identical by hash). d90 large (524288 B) passed 2 of 3 (runs 2b and 2c). In run 2a there was no hidden commit (commits stayed at 2 and the notice stayed "Reply ready. Open the app to finish saving."), and a force-stop while hidden lost the reply silently, with no interrupted note and no notice. The cause was not found. **Lane A did not review this result before stage 2C was committed (`4c6f755b7`, log line 822); the maintainer was told on 2026-10-27.** The maintainer's MVP (`f0e397aae`) predates stage 2C, so the MVP is unaffected.
- **Residual stalls (accepted lists in `MC-426`, `MC-427`):** follow-up CHORE-452, scope INFERRED.
- **OPEN (2026-10-27, ledger row 4506): the stage 2C large-reply hidden-commit failure (run 2a).** A 524288 B non-streamed reply showed no hidden commit and was lost silently on a force-stop while hidden (1 of 3 large runs; the small and 300 s runs passed). ### CHORE-178 — Lorebook `searchMatch` slices from `length - scanDepth` with no clamp, so a scan depth larger than the message count drops the oldest messages

**Status (2026-10-25, lane A records batch 12, from lane G's records):** fixed on `worktree-Lane-G` in `d4e868da7`, technically accepted, merged to `main` as `dceab1844` (pushed). `searchMatch` clamps the scan window's start at 0, and a non-number or negative `@@scan_depth` returns `false`, so the configured depth stays and a `@@@scan_depth` fallback applies (`MC-540` 1 and 2). New test file `lorebookScanDepth.svelte.test.ts`: 13 tests, 6 reproducers fail on `df408f8de` and pass after, 7 guards pass before and after. Non-goal: a NaN or negative configured depth is not validated. Lane G's records do not state administrative closeout, so the ticket stays open here.

**Status (2026-10-24, lane A records batch 10):** open; assigned to lane G's parity batch with CHORE-45 and CHORE-41 (`MC-503`). Source check by the batch 10 doc-verifier pass (reasoned, not run): there is no clamp; with the default depth of 5, a chat of 3 or 4 messages scans only the last 2 or 1.

**Status (2026-10-11): open; not planned; present in upstream too.** Type: suspected defect (lorebook scan, plain and regex keys).

- **Observed (source reading at `e75b1249`, `src/ts/process/lorebook.svelte.ts`, `searchMatch`):** the scanned window is `messages.slice(messages.length - arg.searchDepth, messages.length)` with no clamp of the start.
- **Source and inference:** the follow-up in `Agents/Upstream-Merges.md` for the merge `1f455c0b` says a depth above the message count gives a negative start and drops the oldest messages (for example 3 messages with depth 5 keeps only the last 2) and that it affects plain keys as well as regex keys. That example is reasoning from `Array.prototype.slice` semantics; this pass did not execute it. Not tested.
- **Not decided:** whether to fix it here or upstream; how often a depth above the message count occurs in practice.

Texts: `store2ImportSizeWarning` and `store2ImportTimeWarning` in `src/lang/en.ts` (both contain that sentence; opened 2026-10-21).

- **Problem (as the verifier states it):** the import copies assets before the commit that switches the profile, and writes `assets/<name>` unconditionally, so a same-name asset with different bytes overwrites the previous profile's file before the switch. In that case the sentence is false. `backuplocal.ts` still writes each asset key with `'unconditional'` (opened 2026-10-21); the order relative to the commit is the verifier's reading and was not reopened for this record.
- **Open, `TODO(evidence)`:** how often a name repeats with different bytes. `saveAsset` names assets by SHA-256 or a custom id (verifier claim 6), and CHORE-182 records "content-named additions". CHORE-182's "Why the number matters" bullet says "'not changed until the import has finished' holds by construction but is untested". For a same-name asset with different bytes, that sentence and this entry directly contradict each other, because `backuplocal.ts` writes import assets `'unconditional'`.
- **Fix needs:** a wording change in all 7 locales (the translator) or a skip-if-present-and-verify copy (`MC-326` 3 is the verifier's cross-reference; not reopened).
- **Related:** CHORE-182.

- **Corrected facts (lane C, row 3303):** the copy did not slow at screen-off. After `KEYCODE_SLEEP` (20:57:42) it wrote at full rate for about 61 s; the writes fell at 20:58:43 to 46, and the Tauri `getDataDir` bridge calls stopped at 20:58:43 and resumed at 21:07:44, one second after the wake. During the stall almost all sampled app and renderer threads were in state S and none in D (21 sporadic R samples among about 38,900). b1's `power.txt` blocks 2 to 24 are empty from the poll at 20:57:25, before the sleep, so the empty polls are a poller fault, not screen-off behaviour. The "about 90 s" and "all 21 screen-off polls" wording below is replaced by this.
- **Copy path on Android (source read, tauri 2.12.1 / wry 0.57.0 per the main checkout's `Cargo.lock`; the APK's linked version is not confirmed):** each asset batch is an `invoke` sent through `window.ipc.postMessage`. Each Rust command resolves `app_data_dir()` through a blocking UI-thread hop (`PathPlugin.getDataDir`), and its reply is delivered by `evaluateJavascript` on the UI thread. `WryActivity.onPause` calls `WebView.onPause()`. `KeepAliveService` is a `dataSync` foreground service with no wake lock, and the manifest has no `WAKE_LOCK` permission.
- **Plan:** no special build; page-side ring over CDP plus an on-device sampler; runs R0 (PC), R1 baseline screen-off, R2 backgrounded screen on, R3 screen-off whitelisted, R4 runtime `scheduler.yield` override; fixture ceiling 1.25 GiB (`MC-430` 1, an exception to `MC-330` 1 for this rerun only).
- Plan approved by the maintainer to run after CHORE-204 (`MC-337` 7); lane A built the tooling and ran R0 first (see the first status).
- **Related:** `MC-326` 4, `MC-330`, `MC-336`, `MC-430`; CHORE-203.

**Status (2026-10-22) (superseded by the status above): open, not scheduled.** Source: the corrected measurement-day report (`measure2\report.md`, only its "Corrections from doc-verifier" section and the statements it leaves standing) and `measure2\verify.md` (scratch). Debug MEASURE build, second Note 9, runs b1 and b2; each is one run.

- **MEASURED:** with the screen off, the copy stalls. In b1, `ths.log` shows 0.03 to 0.04 MiB/s with the renderer main thread at 0.9%, after about 90 s of disk writes at roughly the screen-on rate (disk-write figures, not copy rates). The "copy 5000" marks came 617 s (b1) and 554 s (b2) after "database decoded", against 80 s with the screen on. Neither run finished with the screen off in 9 to 10 minutes. (superseded by the status above)
- **WITHDRAWN:** background timer throttling as the mechanism. The copy loop in `backuplocal.ts` yields through `yieldToEventLoop` in `src/ts/storage/saveYield.ts`, which prefers `scheduler.yield()`, then `MessageChannel`, and uses `setTimeout(0)` only as a last resort.
- **MISSING:** power evidence. b1's `power.txt` has one populated sample, taken with the screen on, and all 21 screen-off polls are empty. b2 has no power or diskstats file. The Doze and wake-lock claims in the unverified report text have no raw source. (superseded by the status above)
- **Next:** rerun with working power polling and a trace or a no-yield build. `MC-326` 4 (the import keeps running with the screen off) is affected; until the cause is traced, how much is an open question.
- **Status (2026-10-22, later):** rerun planning goes to lane C as its first step (`Agents/Lanes/lane-C-brief.md`). It produces a plan only; lane A runs it on a phone.
- **Related:** `MC-326` 4, `MC-336`; CHORE-203.

### CHORE-221 — A `.bin` commit's salvage can leave out blocks the person did not confirm

**Status (2026-10-24, lane A records batch 10):** open. Per the lane E audit (source reading, doc-verifier-checked with corrections, not run): the reviewer's reading is CONFIRMED (a failed `remotes/` read becomes an omitted block); the store2 import path is STILL PRESENT (low; narrow trigger); the block-era commit path tail is SUPERSEDED (unreachable on store2).

**Status (2026-10-22): open, candidate; not scheduled.** Source: the lane B stage 2 Gate 1 review (`%TEMP%\risu-lane-b\s2\gate1-r1.md`, non-binding note). It is a reviewer's reading, not independently verified by the orchestrator.

- **Problem (reviewer's claim):** the left-out question (`MC-183`) is asked once, over the validity probe's decode. The commit decodes again, and for a block-format file `RisuSaveDecoder` reads `remotes/` from the app store at that time. So the commit's salvage can leave out blocks that the person never confirmed. Since stage 2 the commit also re-reads the entry from the file, so a same-length body change with an intact header (which the header re-check does not detect) falls in the same class.
- **Possible fix named by the reviewer:** refuse as a changed file when the commit's left-out names are not a subset of the confirmed `facts.omitted`.
- **Related:** `MC-183`, `MC-357`; CHORE-103.

Note 9 export measurement (lane B's device request): dispatched on phone 1 (`MC-343` 2); the result is pending.** Ledger rows 2337 and 2349 to 2356.
- **Mechanism, verified** (`%TEMP%\risu-lane-b\c223\packet.md` with `packet-verify.md`):
  - step A, 'export start' to 'export cold collected': `readStubsForExport` reads the 500 characters one at a time; `readUnits` reads each character's pieces with 8 workers, one `read_range` call per piece; each character is re-encoded as JSON;
  - steps B and C: the writes run at about 0.7 to 0.9 MB/s through plugin-fs `writeFile` in append mode with a 4 MiB buffer. That the per-call write cost causes B and C is inferred, not measured;
  - `get_assets_batch` refuses keys outside `assets/`, so batching step A needs Rust.
- **What changed:**
  - **Measurement** (MEASURE builds only, `import.meta.env.DEV || VITE_RISU_MEASURE==='1'`; folded out of release builds). It is appended to the existing export notes in `src/ts/drive/backuplocal.ts`:
    - 'export cold collected' carries the stub read and encode times (`readStubsForExport`, `src/ts/drive/exportInline.ts`) and the `read_range` calls, total time, wall time with any call in flight, and the `store2/` share (`src/ts/storage/tauriByteTransport.ts`);
    - 'export stub and late payloads done', 'export database written' and 'export closed' carry each `writeFile` call's bytes, the time until the call returns its promise, and the wait (`TauriWriter.put`, `src/ts/exportWriters.ts`). Up to 25 calls are listed, otherwise the cheapest and dearest.
    - A failing clock never fails an export or a read. `util/checkReleaseDist.mjs` gains four field markers.
  - **Memory release:** `writeStubPayloadsAndRelease` writes each stub payload and then drops it from the returned array, and clears `cold.stubEntries` once, only after `encodeDatabaseWithLateColdStorage` returns, in both the full and the partial export. **Status (2026-10-23): open, HIGH priority (the maintainer: "I think we should ticket this study as high priority with secondary problems, as it solves lots of core issues"). It is a study, not a rewrite: no implementation follows without the maintainer's decision. It starts after phone 1 part 3 and the CHORE-225 "after" run, whose numbers it uses. Its decision is recorded in lane A's next MC (now `MC-348`).**

**Status update (2026-10-23, after stages (a) to (c) and stage 0 (c) and (d)):** stages (a) to (c) are done and verified. The root hypothesis, "the whole library is one reactive object", is refuted for the growth case: store2 boots a stub for every non-resident character on every backend, and the grid, list and rail mount only what is on screen. It holds literally only when an enabled V2.1 plugin forces every character to load; the maintainer has no V2.1 plugins. The study replaces it with three findings:
- **R1:** save planning runs on the UI thread as whole-scope passes. Phone measurement (one phone): a due module tick takes 1.9-4.0 s of busy main-thread time (before CHORE-225 stage C).
- **R2:** change detection works by reading everything and comparing. This is a deliberate net, and its cost grows with bytes.
- **R3:** modules are always resident and assembled whole (35.6 MiB at boot, re-read by the net).

Ranking (decision memo section 2, with its Corrections, which take precedence): (1) the save planner and idle net, (2) modules always resident, (3) export and import of a 36 GB library on a phone, all three "must deal with eventually"; then patch in place: chat display, grid, plugin permissions. Per-chat residency is deferred, not a small patch (stage 3, expensive to reverse). Not architecturally broken: the store2 on-disk format, the content-named asset store, the Android shell. Memo: `%TEMP%\risu-b715\c205\decision-memo.md`.

Stage 0 (measurement only) is approved as `MC-348` ("Now, PC + one Note 9 run (Recommended)"):
- (c) module-writer trace: verified; no missed module writer; residual gaps need a V2.1 plugin that does not clean up after itself (`%TEMP%\risu-b715\c205\stage0\c-writers-result.md`).
- (d) PC heap snapshots (best-case hardware; one partial dataset of 164 modules; 500 stubs): module data reaches 68.35 MB (a reachable-union size, not a dominator-retained size), 2.035x its 33.58 MB JSON and about 66% of the roughly 103 MB used heap after GC. 56.0 MB is plain V8 data; Svelte proxies about 5.8 MB; the effect objects, their deps, reactions arrays and snapshot closures together 6.4 MB. The 35.6 MiB vs 33.58 MB size mismatch is unreconciled (`%TEMP%\risu-b715\c205\stage0\d-heap-result.md`, verified with corrections).
- (a) the tick split and (b) the net-found counter are with lane B. The one Note 9 run follows lane B's merge (owed).

- **Question (the maintainer, 2026-10-23):** this is a long-horizon question that includes post-MVP work. Which subsystems will have to be dealt with sooner or later because of architectural problems?
- **Lane A's first read (from session context, unverified; not evidence):**
  - **Root:** the whole library is one reactive object in the page's main-thread memory. Many issues are workarounds for this:
    - save planning finds changes by re-encoding everything (CHORE-225);
    - the boot walk, cold storage, stubs and unload keep less of that object resident (`MC-130` is exploratory);
    - the grid loads every avatar;
    - export and import pass all data through the page.
  - **Secondary:**
    - (1) nearly all CPU work is on the main thread (Svelte, saving, card scripts, image work), with no worker architecture;
    - (2) page-to-native transfer is base64 (`writeVia=b64`);
    - (3) the `.bin` backup is one monolithic file, bound by `MC-175` and `MC-223`;
    - (4) card display scripts run on every message on every pass, under upstream semantics (no caching or limit design);
    - (5) the plugin permission flow re-prompts after each "No".
  - **Judged not architecturally broken so far:** the store2 on-disk format, the content-named asset store, and the Android shell.
- **Study shape:**
  - (a) One investigator per subsystem maps it against library growth, using the maintainer's real sizes: 500+ characters, characters 132 MiB together, 100+ modules (asset modules of 1-2 GB), 383k assets and 32 GiB, a ~36 GB `.bin`. For each, it states when it fails, the cost of changing it, the upstream-compatibility constraints, the tests that would carry over, and whether the current MVP work points the same way.
  - (b) An independent `senior-advisor` challenge on the result, with a separate challenge for the in-memory library model (the expensive-to-reverse one).
  - (c) A short decision memo for the maintainer.
- **Constraints:** `MC-175` and `MC-223` (backup round-trip with upstream); `MC-011` (no fork userbase); `MC-091` (scope amendments are the maintainer's). AGENTS.md's dirty-tracking warning applies to any change-tracking design.
- **Inputs:** `%TEMP%\risu-b715\phone1-fresh\report.md` (part 1, verified); `%TEMP%\risu-lane-e\grid\phone-analysis\report.md` (grid starvation, verified); phone 1 part 3; the CHORE-225 "after" run.

- Scope from the sizing (`%TEMP%\risu-b715\v21-retire\sizing-result.md`; its Corrections take precedence):
  - V1 and V2 already never execute; new V2.1 imports and updates are already refused at three call sites.
  - Seven call sites: bootstrap, ModuleMenu, archiveGate, loadPlugins, bootArchivePass, database wrapModuleAssets, characterPutBack.
  - Stages 1 and 2 land together. Stage 1 alone is incoherent (an enabled V2.1 plugin disables archiving but never runs). Stage 1 also drops the loadPlugins block that unwraps modules, restores all and writes `enabled` off.
  - Keep the pluginV2 handler-set cleanup (the `.clear()` calls and the unload loop), because V3 relies on it.
  - Required test: updating a V2.1 plugin to 3.0 carries the old `enabled` (`carrySavedValues`), so an enabled V2.1 plugin runs again at once. This is the intended migration.
  - Stage 3 (optional) removes only 2.1-only members of the API object. Read `Object.keys(oldApis)` first; the V3 compatibility shims stay.
  - Size: about 12-14 source files edited and 4 deleted; roughly -900 source lines and +60; tests roughly -3,000 and +250; 7 locale files. Stage 2 touches the persistence surface (opus-reviewer).
- Data kept: the `.bin` round trip keeps plugin data byte-for-byte, including the `enabled` flag (MC-349 notes).
- Open UI questions for the maintainer: the boot notice; the toggle for a V2.1 entry; the localized refusal text; the dead `PluginAlertModal` (no non-test code sets its `open`; deleting `pluginSafety.ts` needs its type import removed and the modal's fate decided).
- Moot once CHORE-206 lands (not closed): CHORE-30 and CHORE-65, both V2.1 paths. A title search found only these two; body-text mentions were not swept (TODO(evidence)).

### CHORE-207 — off-main-thread asset work (candidate)

**Status (2026-10-24): open, candidate, not scheduled, no code (`MC-478`).** Filed from the lane A question on carrying the off-main-thread idea forward. The idea's origin is the maintainer's recollection of an advisor's side-chat suggestion to move non-critical work such as asset loading to another thread; no advisor transcript was found, and none is claimed.

- **Findings (verified; confidence as stated):**
  - Existing workers: the Pyodide worker (`scriptings.ts`), Monaco and pdf.js (CONFIRMED). fflate async in cold storage and `.charx` import is possibly already off-thread (INFERRED from fflate's design, not checked in `node_modules`). fflate sync in `bergamotTranslator.ts` and `risuSaveV0.ts` runs on the main thread (CONFIRMED).
  - Rust has no asset decode, resize, hash or zip command (CONFIRMED). Rust has other commands (network, Python, inference), which are not asset work.
  - On Tauri and Node, `getFileSrc` returns native URLs, so no asset bytes cross the main thread for display (CONFIRMED, `globalApi.svelte.ts:355-379`).
  - The recorded main-thread cost is the avatar first-pass `renderThumb`: full-size decode plus `toDataURL` on the main thread (rows 3562 and 3566; MEASURED on PC, phone attribution INFERRED).
  - The plain-HTTP base64 figure of about 8.75 ms CPU per MB is i9-13900K best case (the Roadmap figure; its source cite is stale, now `globalApi.svelte.ts:459`).
  - Not a candidate: `.bin` / `risuSave` decode. This is inference: structured clone copies the object graph and doubles peak memory, and it belongs to lane B.
  - Grid fling jank is not a worker candidate (row 1654 reports a compositor-stage cause; that row is NOT independently verified, and "a worker would not fix it" is the investigator's inference).
- **Ranking (opinion where stated):**
  1. avatar thumbnails;
  2. the plain-HTTP asset path, where a blob URL is likely better than a worker (opinion);
  3. `.charx` import (low).
- **First stage proposed (not approved):** a pure byte-to-thumbnail function using `createImageBitmap` resize and `OffscreenCanvas.convertToBlob`, run on the main thread behind a flag and measured on a Note 9 with a cold synthetic profile, before any worker.
  - It goes beyond grid A2, which is already measured and not shipped (row 3566). The `convertToBlob` stage also changes the output from the current data URL to a Blob.
  - The A2 re-evaluation with the phone capture comes first.
- **UNVERIFIED:**
  - worker PSS on the Note 9;
  - WebP `convertToBlob` on WebView 155;
  - service-worker interaction;
  - the `strip` plugin on worker code.
- **Next:** read `%TEMP%\risu-lane-e\grid\plan.md` to settle the A2 overlap.
- **Related:** the Grid avatar loading entry, MC-452, MC-459, MC-463, MC-130, CHORE-205, CHORE-177.

### CHORE-211 (new) — The preset diff modal throws a TypeError on open when a preset has no `promptTemplate`

**Status (2026-10-25, lane A records batch 14): open, not scheduled; filed by lane A.** Type: defect (an unhandled TypeError in a modal), no data loss, no saving involved. Pre-existing: not caused by the Android Back batch.

- **Observed (phone 2, release build of `31b050a63`, synthetic profile; `%TEMP%\risu-b715\backA\device\result.md`):** four uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')`, one for each of four opens of the preset diff modal ("Show Prompt Comparison", the two compare icons). Back still closed the modal. The device report called the cause UNCERTAIN.
- **Traced (investigator, `%TEMP%\risu-b715\backA\device-followup\result.md`; the load-bearing lines reopened for this entry):** `PromptDiffModal.svelte` `getPromptCards` clones `db.botPresets[id].promptTemplate` and loops over `formated.length`. `promptTemplate` is optional on the preset type (`database.svelte.ts`), so a preset without one gives `undefined`, and `null` gives the same error with "null" in the message. The synthetic generator's presets carry no `promptTemplate`. A real preset saved without a template stores `null` (`saveCurrentPreset` area: `normalizePromptTemplate(db.promptTemplate) ?? null`); imported and upstream legacy presets can also lack it (INFERRED from reading, not run against a real preset). The stage B diff has no hunk touching `getPromptCards` (investigator, `git diff 31b050a63~1 31b050a63`).
- **User impact (INFERRED, not observed):** the exception is thrown inside a derived value while the modal mounts, so a preset without a template probably shows no cards. Exact rendering was not observed.
- **Smallest fix (the investigator's opinion, not a decision):** treat a missing or null `promptTemplate` as an empty list in `getPromptCards`, with a test using a preset that lacks one.
- **Related:** CHORE-352 (COMPLETE, in the closed archive).

### CHORE-212 (new) — Firefox on Android: a full backup from the web or Node build fails ("download failed"); the StreamSaver download stream errors before the prompt is answered

**Status (2026-10-25, lane A records batch 14): open, owner lane A; the cause is not established; next step a debugger-off run with a screen-based prompt detector.** Type: defect on the web and Node builds (a full `.bin` export cannot be saved in mobile Firefox). Decisions: `MC-514` (the maintainer's answers and the choice of remote debugging). The maintainer's report: on risuai.xyz "you have to click yes to the download as soon as it pops up for backup to succeed" (a late answer stalls the export).

- **Present before lane F's work (lane A's check of the raw files, `%TEMP%\risu-b715\ff-baseline\result.md`):** the full backup fails identically on `f3ab26c4a` (the parent of the export Cancel commit) and on `6625baedf` (main before any lane F change), Firefox 158.0.2, phone 2, a Node server on `http://127.0.0.1` over `adb reverse`, an answer 0.4 to 0.6 s after the prompt. Caveat: that origin counts as a secure context; the maintainer's LAN or VPN host is not reproduced.
- **Not caused by the same-origin helper (lane A's verification of `%TEMP%\risu-b715\ff-swap\result.md`):** the fork's own build with only the helper URL swapped to the cross-origin stock helper (served 200) still failed 2 of 2 when answered within 0.45 s; no file landed. The fork serves its vendored helper same-origin only on secure pages (`b6d8e9634`, 2026-10-05; plain HTTP keeps the `github.io` helper).
- **Mechanism (lane A's verification of `%TEMP%\risu-b715\ff-debug\result.md`, Firefox remote debugging, fork build):** the StreamSaver service-worker download stream errors before the prompt is answered (the console shows "A ServiceWorker intercepted the request and encountered an unexpected error" at the vendored `sw.js` `respondWith(new Response(stream, …))` line, ahead of both the prompt and the answer); the page then throws `AbortError` from the export writer's `write`, and the app reports an incomplete backup file. The rejected write was a small entry, so the stream had already been cancelled; nothing large was being written.
- **Upstream fails the same way with the debugger attached:** the upstream build (`f50b5942e`, cross-origin stock helper) produced the same signature in the control run UC1 (239 s to the prompt; the error pair came before the answer). So the signature is not specific to the fork.
- **Unresolved:** why upstream passed 2 of 2 without the debugger (`%TEMP%\risu-b715\ff-compare\result.md`, UZ1 (137.7 MB file, checked by lane A: 137,728,879 B in the `ls` listing) and UZ2 (reported, not size-checked by lane A); answered 0.5 s after the prompt, 36 s and 31 s to the prompt). The debugger attachment and the much longer time to the prompt are a possible confound; the fork-versus-upstream difference with debugging off is unexplained. A late answer (30 s or more) stalls the export in upstream too (a 192 KB partial file).
- **Next step:** a debugger-off run, fork and upstream, answered within 1 s, using a screen-based prompt detector (a screenshot pixel check or `dumpsys window`) in place of the `uiautomator` text poll, which was blind to the prompt with debugging off. Phone 2, synthetic data, Wi-Fi off.
- **Not shown:** whether the fork's vendored `streamSaver.ts` or the first-write timing matters (INFERRED candidates only).
- **Related:** CHORE-62 (the export Cancel closes the stall, but cannot make the download succeed); `MC-514`; `MC-528` to `MC-530`.

### CHORE-227 (new) — After a finished export the app can stay marked busy, and the app has been killed after a second export

**Folded in from lane B's records (merge `91d4c2973`), 2026-10-25, lane A records batch 14.**

**Status (2026-10-25): open, cause unknown (`MC-489`).** Ledger rows 3040 to 3042 (lane B's rows).

- **Observed:** in r3's check C on both phones, the reload-first decision probe read `work.busy:true`, `work.inFlight:true` and `chatSeen:false` at the second Full backup. That came after a finished, OK-closed in-place export and one character open (release `c7c6f94d4`, synthetic 500-character profile).
- **Later kills:** in all three such sequences (r2 C2, and r3 C2 on both phones) the app was later SIGKILLed while in front. `busy:true` was read only in r3; r2 recorded only the missing prompt.
  - phone 1: about 113 s after the second Save, leaving a 0-byte file and a blank page;
  - phone 2: about 26 s after OK, while idle;
  - r2: about 37 s after Save.
  The logs name no cause, and no memory sample covers any kill.
- **Not reproduced:** a clean rerun on phone 1 (`2026-10-25-c205-busy-holder.result.md`; lane A read all 22 CDP JSONs), 2 runs, read busy and in-flight as `[]` at every point after OK: 0, 2, 5 and 15 s, after the character open and return, and at the second decision. r3's history differs: earlier exports in the process, a generation attempt, and a 30 s wait on Success before OK.
- **Source reading (lane B row 3041):** the export's own `backupSave` entry ends in `finally`, and nothing in the export runs after Success. Opening a character starts no busy producer. Every busy entry also holds an in-flight token.
- **Effect while stuck (row 3041, by reading):** Load Local and Load Internal backup are refused; store2 auto-GC, the GC command, manual cleanup and inlay cleanup refuse; the Android keep-alive service stays up. The busy entry has no age limit; its in-flight token expires after 2 h.
- **Next observation:** read the busy kinds over CDP after r3's exact history: a 30 s wait on Success, earlier exports in the same process, and a generation.
- **Side finding, not decided:** `SaveLocalBackup` takes `backupSave` before the save picker opens, against the `busyActions` header comment, so a picker that never answers would hold busy. "Export Save as Dataset" shows no notice and builds the whole file on the page thread.

### CHORE-372 (new) — Lift the self-hosted Node server's 100 MiB limit for assets

**Folded in from lane F's records (merge `95a24de3e`), 2026-10-25, lane A records batch 14.**

- Status (2026-10-27, lane A records batch 15): stages 1 to 7c are on `main` (last `ca84ad6ba`); open. Decisions: `MC-531` to `MC-539` and `MC-750` to `MC-760` (lane F's `F-MC-1` to `F-MC-11`). Phone acceptance on the Note 9 spares: 7a-2 to 7a-5 PASS; 7b FAIL, then fixed as the d1 change in 7c; 7c PASS for the checks run (control build `d751c663b` with the preview ON and 60.55 MiB PNGs, n=1 per cell: 3 images survived, 4 survived at 247.5 MiB of the 256 MiB Java heap, and the fifth crashed the app with 2 out-of-memory log lines in the same second; the AFTER build peaked at 65.7 MiB with 0 such lines; `c372-s7c\result.md`; the emotion-in-chat half of check d was not completed). The phone-2 send TypeError seen during the run is an out-of-range `fmIndex` (CHORE-456), identical on CONTROL and AFTER. Still owed: the preview-ON list and module-list checks at 12 x 6 MiB and a refused-restore run with the MEASURE line. Lane F's ledger rows after 3787 and the per-stage approvals for 7a-1 to 7a-5 are not in lane F's records: TODO(evidence). Follow-ups: CHORE-373 to 375, 462, 219.
- Status (2026-10-25, Lane F): open; sizing only, after batch 2 (`MC-524`). No code.
- What Lane F's source read found (TRACED, orchestrator, `8003b3c4c`; not yet independently verified):
  - One constant, `NODE_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES` (104857600), defined in `src/ts/storage/nodeBodyLimit.ts` and `server/node/bodyLimit.cjs` and pinned equal by a test, bounds every Node body: the server's json, raw and text parsers and `/hub-proxy` (`server/node/server.cjs`), the block store and main-file checks (`blockStore.ts`, `bootArchivePass.ts`, `mainFileRename.ts`), cold storage, internal backup, the save-loop message, and the asset pre-checks (`processzip.ts`, `inlayStore.ts`, `backuplocal.ts` restore). Raising it everywhere would let non-asset bodies grow too.
  - The write handler buffers the whole body (`express.raw`), then writes a temp file and renames it; the revision is bumped before the content is written.
  - The asset route serves files with `res.sendFile` (streamed); `/api/read` reads the whole file into memory (a comment there says it avoids `sendFile` because of a lock).
- Work to size: a separate asset limit applied on the asset write route only; streaming that upload into the temp file, with the revision-before-content order re-argued (persistence risk, `opus-reviewer`); moving the client pre-checks and messages to the new limit; any large-asset reads through `/api/read`; client memory for one large upload body (how store2 uploads an asset was not traced); and the fork-to-upstream export warning and omission for assets over upstream's limit (`MC-223`), which on web and Node is CHORE-129's parked residual (`MC-501`).
- Unverified: which callers read assets through `/api/read`; how store2 sends an asset body.

### CHORE-354 (new) — on a large profile the page does not answer Back within 1 s after the first Back, and no hide event fires on the quit

**Status (2026-10-25, lane A records batch 12):** out of lane A's Android Back batch; it needs a profile first (lane A ruling recorded in `MC-505`).

On phone 2 with the large profile `m2-mix18k` (CHORE-181 cell 5), after the first Android Back showed the quit toast the page logged nothing further and the toast never cleared; the second Back quit only through the host's 1 s timeout (1.018 s, against 16 to 30 ms on the small profile). The edits persisted. On both large-profile quits no `visibilitychange` to hidden fired (0 of 2, against 5 of 5 on the small profile; `pagehide` 0 of 7 overall), so save-on-hide (CHORE-153) did not run and the Back-guard flush (CHORE-181) was the only save request before the quit.

- Candidate causes (neither shown): (a) the Back-guard flush's save, only if `hasUnsavedSaveMarks()` was true at the first Back (in run r1 the edit was 3.4 s old, so the flush was probably a no-op; only run r2-fast could involve it); (b) the debounced save or other large-profile main-thread work. Not shown to be a CHORE-181 regression.
- Evidence needed: a page CPU profile across the first and second Back on `m2-mix18k`; a matched large-profile run with the flush disabled and the edit within about 100 ms of the first Back. Removing or deferring the flush is not supported without that run, since it is the only pre-quit save request when no hide event fires.
- Cost bounded: the second Back still quits through the host timeout; no edit was lost in the runs.

### CHORE-370 — V3 `setCharacterToIndex` may not mark its write for saving (new)

**Folded in from lane F's records (merge `4331440b6`), 2026-10-25, lane A records batch 12.**

- Status (2026-10-24, Lane F): open, in this batch (`MC-520` 4). Found by the CHORE-30 sizing (TRACED, not run): it assigns `DBState.db.characters[charId]` directly and does not call `setDatabaseLite`, unlike `setChar`. Whether the write is saved is unverified; the check comes first.
- Status (2026-10-25, Lane F): premise refuted; no fix needed (`MC-520` 4 called for a fix only if the write is not saved). The sizing's premise that `setChar` marks characters through `setDatabaseLite` was wrong: the `setDatabaseLite` that `setChar` calls (`src/ts/storage/database.svelte.ts`) only wraps module asset lists and assigns `DBState.db`; it marks no characters. Both `setChar` and `setCharacterToIndex` are saved by the identity tracker in `src/ts/storage/dbChangeEffects.svelte.ts`, which marks any replaced element of `DBState.db.characters` by `chaId`; its comment names V3 `setCharacterToIndex` (TRACED). The existing guard `src/ts/plugins/apiV3/tests/v3SaveMarks.svelte.test.ts` drives `setCharacterToIndex` through the real effects and the real encoder round trip: 2 passed, run by Lane F on `df408f8de` on 2026-10-25 (EXECUTED). Packet: the investigator's hand-back, with `%TEMP%\risu-lane-f\save-mark\neg1.out.json` for the `-1` case.
- Residual, not sized: when the replacing object carries a different `chaId` from the slot it replaces, the tracker does not delete the old `chaId`'s block (TRACED: the tracker only appends marks). `MC-520` 1 covers `setChar`/`setCharacter` only, so `setCharacterToIndex` still accepts a different `chaId`. Whether the old block comes back on reload was not checked. TODO(evidence).

### CHORE-374 (new) — Inlay object URLs carry an untrusted MIME type at the app origin

**Folded in from lane F's records, 2026-10-27.** Follow-up to CHORE-372 stage 6. An inlay restored from a backup is rebuilt as a Blob with the backup header's `mime`, and `deliverStoredInlay` hands it to `URL.createObjectURL`; a `blob:` URL has the page's origin, so an inlay typed as SVG or HTML opened in its own tab could run script with access to the app's storage (TRACED from source by the Stage 6 Gate 1 reviewers; not run in a browser; pre-existing). Next: a MIME-string allowlist for inlay object URLs (raster image, video, audio; everything else `application/octet-stream`), framed as integrity on the private hosted build. Also (Stage 6b-1 Gate 2 N1): in pretranslate mode a `blob:` URL in message HTML changes every page load, so the translation cache key misses after a reload.

**Status (2026-10-28, lane A records batch 18): stages A and B merged; stage C split into C0 to C3 and in Gate 1 round 4; not COMPLETE.** Decisions: `MC-579` (items 1 to 9; narrows `MC-576` item 3 so that only CharX streams), `MC-790` (amends `MC-579` item 9: `x_meta` type-only above 64 MiB in CharX), `MC-791` (stage C: progress with the asset name and written and total MiB; Cancel on desktop and Android CharX export, a partial file after a cancel is kept with the existing "file is incomplete" notice; a failed export stops the web download, and when part was written the error says the file is incomplete; no extra notice for large images exported uncompressed), `MC-792` (platform-neutral incomplete-file notices, the translator updates the six other locales; on Android a returned `content://` save target counts as saved), `MC-793` (amends `MC-792` item 1: fix the CharX writer and the importer; keep the notice condition), `MC-794` (extends `MC-791` item 1: two-phase progress, "Checking {name}" then "Writing {name}", and a refusal when the entry count could pass 65,535) and `MC-795` ("Refuse up front": the export pre-check refuses a CharX with any asset above the fork's CharX import cap, naming the asset). All were confirmed by the maintainer in lane A on 2026-10-28 ("Yes, record it" or "Yes, record them"). **Stage A** (asset piece sources move to `drive/assetPieces.ts`) is `69f4700ca` (lane G's `399d45b3f`; standing approval for stage A only, "Merge when clean (Recommended)"; trial `91bbe2080` on `4ad6ece37`: 25,538 passed, 5 failed = CHORE-466 x2, CHORE-410 x2, CHORE-464 x1; 158 of 158 alone, three times). **Stage B** (the size pre-check and refusals) is `f0e320692` (lane G's `9925b0da9`; the commit subject: exports with an asset above 64 MiB are checked before anything opens; PNG, JSON, `.risum` and Realm refuse them, CharX refuses near 4 GiB; `MC-500` approval "Merge and push (Recommended)"; trial `ae0ba7838`: 25,583 passed, 4 failed in known classes, CHORE-466 x2 with the `selIdState` error and CHORE-410 x2; the files alone 145 of 145; check, build and dist 0; tree equal, `51aae9dc`). **Stage C** Gate 1 had three substantive REJECT rounds (as recorded with `MC-795`), then the advisor escalation: no mechanism change. Finding behind `MC-793` (lane G's re-check of a Gate 1 probe, not independently verified by lane A; the senior-advisor confirmed the premise from its own runs): fflate's streaming `Zip` always writes a data descriptor, and the streaming `Unzip` (the fork's CharX importer and upstream's) ends such an entry at the first zip signature inside its data, so deflate level 0 and STORE both break; this is pre-existing in today's CharX export and in upstream. The reviewer's estimate is about 5% of 64 MiB, 20% of 300 MiB and 53% of 1 GiB random-looking assets (an estimate, not measured here). The `MC-777` "Simpler path" (STORE entries) was therefore rejected by a lane G probe. Plan v4: test-only C0 (today's framing as a fixture helper, descriptor tests moved, stage A pins reproduced), then C1 (own zip framing with CRC and sizes in each local header; fixes the existing signature bug), C2 (large assets stored, two passes), C3 (Tauri Cancel, notices, dismissed dialog), each its own Gate 2 and `MC-500` merge; CHORE-394 follows C1; D (thumbnails, `MC-576` item 4) is separate. Round 4, the plan v4 review, is [EDITORIAL] (lane A's coordinator message, 2026-10-28): the plan is accepted, the REJECT streak ended at three, and C0 is next; the verdict file is not cited here: TODO(evidence). **Open note, assets above 1 GiB on Tauri and Android stores (not ticketed; lane G from a code read, not verified by lane A):** a `.bin` restore applies the per-asset limit only on Node (`nodeRestoreAssetLimit`), and the MVP `f0e397aae` had none, so a Tauri store can hold an asset above 1 GiB. The `.bin` export streams such assets (only the 4 GiB per-entry container cap, named by the pre-flight); PNG, JSON, `.risum` and Realm refuse above 64 MiB; CharX refuses above the import cap (`MC-795`). UNTRACED: opening or displaying such an asset in the UI, and re-importing a `.bin` holding one on a non-Node store.

**Status (2026-10-27, lane A records batch 17): part B of umbrella CHORE-219; owner lane G (from lane F; `MC-576`).** Lane F has no live session, so no message was sent; the handover is recorded here and in [Lanes/README.md](Lanes/README.md). `MC-576` decides streamed exports (writers take slices, not whole reads) and bounded thumbnails above 64 MiB (amends `MC-756` for thumbnails).

### CHORE-413 (new) — The Playground's module waits (Subtitle and ImageTrans) are inert: with no selected character the wait loads nothing

**Folded in from lane B's records (`Agents/Lanes/lane-B-records.md`, committed in `43310c81e`), lane A records batch 18.** **Status (2026-10-27): open, new; filed by lane B at the maintainer's answer recorded with `MC-668`.** The Playground has no selected character: `PlaygroundSubtitle` and `PlaygroundImageTrans` render only while `selectedCharID < 0`, so `getCurrentCharacter()` is undefined, `ensureCurrentModules` returns true before it resolves an origin, and the waits S4d-1p-4b1 added at both sites load nothing (read in source at `523ca8ac4`, not executed). `{{lorebook}}` and `{{worldinfo}}` cannot show a stub difference there; `{{moduleassetlist::ns}}` can, but the default Subtitle prompts do not use it, so the exposure needs a hand-edited prompt during or after a failed background load of a globally enabled stub. A real fix needs an ensure for the no-selection reference set, a design change. Open for sizing: what `requestChatData` reads from modules when nothing is selected (uncertain; it decides whether the inert wait matters); Iris opened with no character is inert in the same way. Lane B's reading, not the maintainer's words: no S4d-1p action. Related: `MC-668`, `MC-655`, `MC-658`, CHORE-205, CHORE-414.

### CHORE-414 (new) — Non-LLM translator types never wait for modules: display regex of a stub module is missed until it loads, and one persisted route exists (an HTML chat export with translation)

**Folded in from lane B's records, lane A records batch 18.** **Status (2026-10-27): open, new; filed by lane B at the maintainer's answer recorded with `MC-668`.** The only module wait is inside `translateLLM`; the google, deepl, deeplX and bergamot paths never wait, and the DOM path and `applyEdittransRegex` read module regex scripts with no subject, which skips stubs. In the chat view this is temporary (a module load bumps the GUI pointer and remounts `ChatBody`; traced, not run). One persisted route: `exportChat` as HTML with "translate content", a non-LLM translator and `combineTranslation` runs `processScriptFull` on the translated text and writes it into the downloaded file, so a stub's display regex missing at that moment would be baked into the file; it needs a user-initiated export during the load window or after a failed load. The saved chat is untouched and no data-loss path was found. The scope call (the `MC-655` wording covers LLM requests) is the maintainer's. Read in source at `523ca8ac4`, not executed. Related: `MC-668`, `MC-655`, `MC-658`, CHORE-205, CHORE-413.

### CHORE-415 (new) — `Chat.svelte`'s `getTranslationCacheKey` builds the translation editor's cache key without the translator's module wait

**Folded in from lane B's records, lane A records batch 18.** **Status (2026-10-28): open, new; filed by lane B (the F7 finding carried as a "ticket candidate" in the S4d-1p-4c plan, Gate 1 and Gate 2 records and the `14c77f3ed` commit message).** An editor opened during the load window seeds from, and saves to, the pre-load key; after the load `ChatBody` reads the post-load key, so the edit is not shown. It needs a user who opens the translation editor while the message view is still waiting; the reach is the display of the user's own edit only, and the reviewer did not require a fix for 4c. Lane B re-read `Chat.svelte` at `4ad6ece37`: the key is still built with no module wait. To size: whether the key builder should use the same `waitForTranslatorModules()` call as `ChatBody` and what the editor shows if the wait fails (`MC-658`, `MC-662`, `MC-667`). Related: `MC-668`, `MC-669`, CHORE-205 S4d-1p-4c.

- **CHORE-477 — the `assetPieceSave` unhandled `AssetStorageFullError` is a test bug. Fixed by the flake batch; pending later full runs.** A maintainer-run account 2 cloud investigation (read-only, `caa1f842d`) found it and lane A verified the key claim from source. In `src/ts/storage/tests/assetPieceSave.test.ts`, the test "reproducer: saveAsset turns an empty quota error into the storage-full message and names the file only when it has an extension" starts three `saveAsset` calls (named, extensionOnly, unnamed) and awaits `named` first; the other two can reject before their handlers attach (`crypto.subtle.digest` finishes out of order), and vitest 4.1.12 never retracts an unhandled rejection. Reproduced alone in 10 of 20 runs (70 of 70 tests pass each time, 13 unhandled rejections, all "unnamed file"); a plain-Node model gave 119 of 2,000 against 0 with the handlers attached at once. Fix (test-only): `Promise.allSettled` over the three calls. Not a product bug on this path; real UI callers of `saveAsset` were not audited. The maintainer folded all flaky-test fixes into one batch (`MC-779`): CHORE-477, CHORE-410, CHORE-466, `backendIndexedDb` "hazard is real", `saveYieldMeter` and `nodeRetire`, with one Gate 2 and one trial merge. ### Grid avatar loading (MC-452, MC-459) — stage A, the thumbnail write leaves the generation slot (no CHORE number)

**Status (2026-10-23, lane A records batch 4):** grid stage B1' is merged in `f16fb5c30` (lane E; 21,046 passed; hashes matched Gate 2; `MC-463`, `MC-464`). The rest of stage B is deferred (`MC-463`). Lane E is retired for now. The grid capture on a re-import is owed; lane A runs it.

**Status (2026-10-22, lane A fold-in): stage A1 merged in `771ec2b30` (no-ff of `cd4546b8e`; `MC-459` to `MC-462`); full checks on `771ec2b30`: `pnpm test` 20,968 passed, 23 skipped; `pnpm check` 0/0; build passed. The phone 1 cold capture was taken on a pre-A1 build (lane E's ledger rows 3562 to 3570; the grid part of `%TEMP%\risu-b715\phone1-fresh\report.md`, whose thumbnail counts are unsupported). Stage B waits on lane E's trace analysis.** Decisions: MC-459, MC-460 and MC-461.

- In `src/ts/media/avatarThumb.ts`, a finished thumbnail is memoized, its waiters resolve and its concurrency slot is freed before its IndexedDB write settles; the write runs outside the slot, still bounded by the store timeout, and its failure stays silent. A slow store therefore no longer holds back the next thumbnail.
- Backpressure: no new task starts while 4 or more writes are unsettled. Tasks already running still add their writes, so the peak is 5 at the default concurrency of 2 (probe, row 3568). A write that times out stops counting even though the underlying IndexedDB request may still be pending (accepted, row 3568).
- Test hooks: `setLimits` accepts `maxPendingWrites`; `reset()` clears the pending-write count and a write epoch keeps a late write from an earlier test out of a later count; `stats()` exposes the count.
- Existing test T7b changed deliberately: a write timeout no longer "frees the slot"; it now asserts that generation resumes once the pending write times out (with a cap of 1).
- A2 (decode through `fetch` and `createImageBitmap`) is not shipped: it did not meet the pre-set 30% rule (row 3566). `THUMB_VERSION` is unchanged, so existing thumbnails stay valid.
- Not measured on a device; the PC figures are best-case hardware. Whether stage A changes the phone fill rate is unknown until the phone capture.
- Stage B (from the Gate 1 verdict, `%TEMP%\risu-lane-e\grid\g1\verdict.md`, B-1 to B-8): read timeout not a miss, no full-size paint for big images (grid avatar component only, unknown size counts as big), dropping queued work nobody waits for; retry rules per MC-461 (fill on next draw; a few retries with back-off, then grey until restart).

### CHORE-200 — On a Node server, a cold-storage unit over the request body limit fails only after the upload (fixed in stage 2a; the boot-pass strike for an oversize unit remains open as a proposed follow-up)

**Status (2026-10-24, lane A records batch 10): PARKED (`MC-501`, group A) for the residual only.** The Node boot-pass strike for an oversize cold unit is parked. Can return on a maintainer decision.

**Status (2026-10-12): fixed in stage 2a (`bd02c04d4`, merged in `2e065403a`; ledger rows 2024, 2027 to 2030); the boot-pass strike for an oversize unit remains open as a proposed follow-up, not scheduled.**

- **Problem as filed (found during card import sizing, B2; ledger row 2009):** `setColdStorageItem` has no request-body size check on the Node server. A unit over the server's body limit fails only after the upload. Card import falls back to a live card, so nothing is lost. Stage 2 re-archives loaded characters, so a large one would repeat that failed upload every time you move on. The filing proposed a size check against the server limit before the write, or having stage 2 remember the failure.
- **What changed in stage 2a (ledger rows 2024 and 2027, the coder's hand-back; Gate 2 `[APPROVE]` at row 2030):** `writeColdStorageUnit` in `coldstorage.svelte.ts` has a Node-only strict `>` guard on the compressed length, a 413 is mapped only for writes, and the archive engine has a non-counting `too-large` reason, so such a character stays loaded, is not retried in the session and does not count toward the two-failures rule (`MC-307` 4; for an import batch `MC-308` 3). Refined premise (row 2024): the boot pass checked the Node body limit only for commit blocks, and no unit write checked it.
- **Still open (proposed, not filed beyond this note):** a strike in the boot pass for an oversize unit. Gate 2's re-review noted it "matters more under `MC-308` 4" (row 2030, informational).
- **Related:** `MC-307` 2 and 4, `MC-308` 3 and 4, `MC-303` 3; CHORE-201.

Q-B: the maintainer chose "No, accept the limit (Recommended)": a kill within about 1 s of the pass start losing its retry counter is recorded as a known limit; no durable storage. This clears the table's "needs review" for this row; the earlier text below is unchanged, and its "not decided" wording is superseded by this line.

Summary status is in the "Android boot batch" bullet near the top. Everything below is from the session scratch reports named in the ledger rows 1515 to 1549 (`androidboot\`); "session scratch" paths below are relative to it.

**Investigations and their verification.**
- CHORE-137 hang mechanism: `chore137.md` verified by `chore137-verify.md` ("HOLDS WITH CORRECTIONS"; see CHORE-137).
- Why the archive pass was desktop-only, and what Android needs: `archive-pass.md` verified by `archive-pass-verify.md` ("HOLDS WITH CORRECTIONS"). Corrections that mattered: the exclusivity premise is "one live page (JavaScript realm) per store", not "one process"; the pass adds synchronous `plugin:fs|remove` calls before `loadedStore` (the CHORE-137 class, which part 1 removed); and the report's claim that `cleanChunks` reads every unit at startup is refuted (once any character is archived the startup asset sweep is skipped, so orphaned assets accumulate until the manual clean-up). The maintainer's `MC-198` item 2 wording was checked in `archive-pass-verify.md` claim 6.
- Two live pages after an activity recreation: `twopages\report.md` (unverified investigator packet) verified by `twopages\verify.md` (`opus-reviewer`; claims 1 to 3 confirmed, claim 4 "overstated", claim 5 "pre-existing confirmed, widened overstated"). The verifier's finding, traced in source and not executed: on Android the old page's requests reach native code but the replies are evaluated in the replacement WebView, so the old page becomes a zombie whose storage chains stall; unsaved edits in the old page are lost like a process kill (pre-existing); repeated font or density changes could stack zombies and, on a low-memory phone, end in an out-of-memory kill (inferred). Device test C10 then showed two live pages after a font change (Note 9: same pid, two pages after `font_scale` 1.1 to 1.3; the old page kept running, and its `app_fs_exists` call timed out at 15 s, so its IPC never completed, consistent with the verifier's trace).
- Boot baseline on the Note 9: `baseline.md` verified by `baseline-verify.md` (CHORE-161, CHORE-162). Raw location packet `tauri-callsites.md` (code-searcher, ledger row 1519) is an input only, not an independently accepted report: its table is partly garbled in the saved file (the scope list shows `\` for variables) and it was not used as evidence here.
- Font scale options (`nextbatch\fontscale\report.md`): an investigator packet; the key claim (the WebView computes `(int)(fontScale*100)` once, in `updateFontScaleLocked`, at creation, and not after the embedder sets the zoom) was checked by the Orchestrator and again by `opus-reviewer` in `nextbatch\review3.md` against Chromium copies.
- Onboarding after the pass (`nextbatch\onboarding\report.md`): an investigator packet, verified in `nextbatch\review3.md` (claims a to d confirmed; claim e confirmed for the PC files but invalid as a refutation of the device case). See the Orchestrator correction below.
