**Accepted and untested limits.**
- "Back, then relaunch with the process alive" is untested because Back kills the process (CHORE-147); the `9371359e` retire of a leftover WebView after a back-exit rests on the Gate 2 source reading. The retire keeps one finished Activity and its WebView alive after a back-exit until the next creation or process death (Gate 2, Low, informational).
- Not run, by their reports: an asset-heavy module (the profile has no modules); the ModuleMenu multi-file and ToolConversion pickers (pass 1); the upstream `U0.bin` kill points 3 and 4 and stub enrichment; C6 point 2 on the Note 9; a per-character integrity check after C8b; the T0 control for the onboarding screen (restore T0 and relaunch with no font change; step N2 was skipped under its own rule); `always_finish_activities` (inconclusive on the emulator); a recreation caused by a still-undeclared configuration change, on F.
- After `setTextZoom` the WebView no longer follows the system font scale by itself; the app applies it on every configuration change (`nextbatch\fontscale\report.md`; `nextbatch\review3.md`: nothing relies on the automatic follow; the only path disabled is `AwContents.updateContext`, reached only from `adopt()`, which neither wry nor this app calls).
- `fontWeightAdjustment`: the fontscale packet found no WebView reference (uncertain; likely inert). For `uiMode`/`colorMode` the packet confirmed a call into the renderer-preference update but inferred its effect (not exercised on a device); round 2 F2 and F3 showed the density and locale cases working.
- With every file selectable on Android, a pick that does not match the caller's extension list, or whose display name has no extension, is dropped silently to an empty result (the post-pick filter was kept; accepted at Gate 1).
- The unsaved edits of a superseded page are lost (like a process kill); this is pre-existing.
- Device quirk, Note 9 (report wording): `wm density reset` did not clear a density override there; `wm density 420` did.
- Hang counts exist only for pass 1; the Note 9 never reproduced the hang; the emulator runs under arm64 translation.
- Gate 2 round 1 N4: each app command resolves `app_data_dir()` per call (a UI round trip on Android), so a first pass that deletes many blocks costs that many sequential round trips; latency, not a hang; not measured.

**Open maintainer questions (not decided).**
- **Q-A, first-boot cost (`MC-258` 3 "Accept, then judge").** The first boot with the pass on a 1000-character profile took about 144.5 s on the Note 9 and about 225 s on the API 35 emulator, once per profile (n=1 each; the Note 9 text says "launch -> notice"); later boots took about 1.9 s against 8.2 s unarchived on the Note 9, with page memory about 1.27 GB down to about 220 MB. The maintainer's judgement of that cost, and whether any cap or notice on the first boot is wanted, is open. Later Note 9 passes under other conditions were also about 140 s (`device\note9\report.md` C1), and the emulator report names this question "Q-A first-boot size cap".
- **Q-B, durable start strikes.** The archive pass records a start strike in `localStorage` (`archivePassStrikes`) and pauses after two unfinished starts. Device pass 1 step C5: on the Note 9 two force-stops about 1 s after the pass started (3 of 1000) did not persist their strikes and the third boot ran the pass; kills at about 70 s and about 20 s persisted theirs and the next boot showed the paused notice (the Note 9 report marks this inferred from source). On the emulator two kills about 0.3 s after the "Archiving" text appeared lost their strikes; a kill at 235 of 1000 persisted and the next boot paused ("did not finish twice in a row"). A restarted pass adds a strike (the emulator, build E: "1" after the first start, "2" after the recreation) but completes and clears it; with build F a font change no longer restarts it. So a kill within about a second of the start loses its strike and a repeated fast kill loop could retry without bound (the verifier's inference in `archive-pass-verify.md` claim 5, never observed beyond two kills). Question: should strikes be durable (stored where a kill cannot lose them)? Not decided; the commit message of `573b7994` records it as open.

**Unexplained observations, not ticketed.** (a) Emulator, device pass 1 (`device\emu\report.md`, Phase B pickers): a generic single-file cancel on Modules import left a blank dark page for about 8 s after Back, recovered on tap; not investigated. (b) Note 9, device pass 1 (`device\note9\report.md`, Notes): a possible accidental toggle of a character checkbox on and off during the strike reset (synthetic profile).

**Records closeout.** Drafted by `doc-writer` from the reports above and the Orchestrator's brief (ledger row 1550). Records verified by `doc-verifier` 2026-10-09 (PASS WITH FIXES, fixes applied; ledger row 1551); records committed in `ecf06db4`.

### QOL side track (2026-10-12): batch import of plugins and modules, plugin export, per-model data fetched online

**Status (2026-10-12): IN PROGRESS, not COMPLETE; branch `feat/qol` in the worktree `C:\Projects\RisuAI\.claude\worktrees\qol` at `d6717f66`; nothing committed or merged.** Decisions: `MC-280`. Ledger rows 1707, 1708, 1711, 1712 and 1714 to 1718. Everything below is from the session scratch reports in `qol\` (relative to the session scratch directory). The surveys and plans are investigator reports and are not independently verified; the Orchestrator spot-checked the `LLMModel` fields, the default of `dynamicModelRegistry` and the picker and plugin prompts only.

**What the maintainer asked and decided (`MC-280`).** Batch import of modules and plugins, plugin export, and per-model data (max context, pricing, max output) fetched online instead of hand-coded; a separate worktree with a merge after S4-3 (S4-3 is pushed; nothing is merged). The labels chosen are in `MC-280`; the option texts were not saved, so the plans' readings of them are not independently checked against what the maintainer saw (TODO(evidence)).

**Surveys (read-only; premises they refuted, as the surveys state them).**
- `survey-import-export.md`: plugin install has no security review or permission prompt in `importPlugin`, only a duplicate-name confirm (runtime permissions for plugin API V3 are asked later); a multi-file picker is not the missing piece (`selectMultipleFile` and `selectFileByDom(...,'multiple')` exist on all three platforms, but `selectMultipleFile` reads every selected file whole, which is wrong for large `.risum` and `.charx`); a batch runner with the decided failure report (`MC-192` D1) exists for characters but is private to `characterCards.ts`; plugin export does not exist in the fork or in `upstream/main`; a naive loop over `importPlugin` would raise an alert per failure, a confirm per duplicate and run `loadPlugins()` once per file.
- `survey-model-list.md`: a catalog entry has no max-context, max-output or pricing field; max context is one global setting (`db.maxContext`, default 4000), not per model; a dynamic registry already exists and defaults on (`registerModelDynamic`, `db.dynamicModelRegistry`), fetching the Google, Anthropic and OpenAI lists with the user's own keys but keeping only id and display name, in memory, re-fetched every boot; the OpenRouter and NanoGPT pickers already fetch context and prices on demand; no cost display exists; the catalog's flags, format and tokenizer decide how requests are built and no public dataset carries them (the survey's inference). Roughly 176 `id:` lines across the catalog files, including some non-entry lines (not an exact entry count). No prior ticket or MC decision covers the topic (grep result, partial for omitted long lines).

**Plans (as of this closeout: Gate 1 round 1 returned `[REJECT]` for both, amendments A1 to A13 and A1 to A9; revision 2 was applied and round 2 was running when this was written; later gates and dispatches are recorded in the QOL closeout).**
- `plan-batch-import.md` (`investigator`): corrects the survey (the plugin classify helpers do not fit the decided "update with saved settings kept" semantics; a re-imported module can never match by id because every import gets a fresh id, so a duplicate means the same name); a shared batch reporter (`importBatch.ts`), then batch plugin import (one review dialog, one `setDatabaseLite`, one `loadPlugins()`) and batch module import (Replace keeps the existing id, list position, enabled state and references; Skip; Add anyway).
- `plan-model-data.md` (`investigator`): two versioned JSON stores kept on the device outside the `.bin` (limits, pricing), keyed by a canonical model key with equality matching only, an OpenRouter keyless fetch plus the provider lists, manual refresh plus a 24-hour staleness rule; it finds that `registerModelDynamic` would duplicate Google entries on a second call (reasoned from source, not executed), so refresh must split fetching from registration. It lists open questions for the maintainer.

**Plugin export (implemented in the worktree, uncommitted; `plugin-export-report.md`, the coder's own report).** A row button in `PluginSettings.svelte` downloads the stored plugin script as `<name>.js` through `downloadFile`, without the saved argument values; `pluginExport.ts` sanitises the file name (path-hostile characters, Windows reserved names, a fallback); no new language key (it reuses `export`). A probe showed that the `//@` header lines of a plugin imported as `.ts` survive the transpile, so the exported file re-imports with its metadata; the original TypeScript is not retained. Tests: 8 in `pluginExport.test.ts` (including a round trip through the real `importPlugin`) and 2 in `PluginSettings.export.svelte.test.ts`; a targeted run of `src/ts/plugins` and `src/lib/Setting/Pages/PluginSettings` gave 21 files and 444 tests passed, and `pnpm check` 0 errors, 0 warnings; no full `pnpm test` or build. Five scratch mutants (sanitiser keeps `/`, export includes the saved values, no fallback, wrong extension, button wired to the first plugin) each failed 3, 4, 1, 8 and 1 tests. Gate 2 (`adversarial-reviewer`) returned `[EDITORIAL]` (a fixture gap), and the test-only fix is done. Not run: the full suite, the build. The Windows reserved-name handling is small extra scope beyond the brief.

**Requested (`MC-280` 6) and answered (`MC-280` 7 to 9).** Whether plugin settings can be saved in a separate format that only this fork imports, and whether a "fetch model list" feature can replace the hand-maintained dictionary. Reports exist in the session scratch (`qol\survey-plugin-settings.md`, `qol\survey-model-list-fetch.md`; not independently verified) and are not summarised here. The maintainer answered rounds QOL 4 to QOL 6 (labels in `MC-280` 7 to 9); the plugin settings plan was at Gate 1 round 2 when this was written; the model-list fetch question was folded into the model data plan (labels "Harden existing registry" and fetched models "Under 'show all'", meanings from the plans, not independently checked).

**Not decided / owed.** The Gate 1 round 2 outcomes of the plans; the implementation of batch import and per-model data; plugin export full checks, commit on `feat/qol` and merge; the merge of `feat/qol` (the maintainer's words allow it after S4-3; each item still needs its gates); the plans' open questions for the maintainer.

**Owed device and Tauri checks**

- **Stub v3:** on the Note 9 (Android, Tauri) and Windows (Tauri desktop, and a Node server profile), a profile with many version 2 stubs upgrades once at the next startup: the progress line shows, the time per 1000 archived characters is recorded (to decide whether a per-boot cap is needed), a second startup reads nothing, chat counts are unchanged, and searching by tag and by an edited creator finds archived characters. On iOS or an OPFS-only browser, version 2 stubs stay, show the right chat count and are not found by tag (accepted, `MC-302` 2).
- **Card import:** on the Note 9 (Android, Tauri), import about 20 cards including a charx of 20 MB or more. Check there is no out-of-memory, record the time per card, then open, put back and restart. A kill between the import and the next save leaves either no card, or a live card with an orphan archive file. On Windows (Tauri desktop), open-with and drop imports show the stub, write the archive file under AppData and survive a restart, and the `.bin` exports to and re-imports in upstream. On the web, the picker and `#import=` archive on IndexedDB; on a Node server, a card over the body limit falls back to live (CHORE-200); OPFS or no Web Locks imports live; a second tab opened after the first finished booting.
- **Trash archiving:**
  - **Note 9 (Android, Tauri), and Windows (Tauri desktop and a Node server profile):**
    1. Prepare one character trashed today and one with a `trashTime` 4 days old.
    2. Boot: the first gets an archive file; the second is purged and gets none.
    3. The Trash tab shows the archived one with its image, name and description, and search finds it.
    4. Restore it from the trash, then open it: it is not trashed.
    5. Delete it permanently: its archive file stays until "Clean unused cold storage" removes it.
    6. Restart: nothing is archived again.
  - **Web (IndexedDB):** the same steps. (The idle reload this step also checked was removed in item 5.)
  - **Upstream round trip:**
    - Import a fork `.bin` into upstream: the trashed stub shows in upstream's trash. Untrash it, then open it: the character is untrashed. Move the clock 3 or more days forward and restart upstream: the character is still there.
    - Opening a trashed stub on upstream without untrashing it takes it out of the trash. This is expected and lossless.
    - Import an upstream `.bin` back into the fork: the stub keeps its trash state.
- **Stage 2a:**
  - **Note 9 (Android, Tauri), Windows (Tauri desktop) and the web (IndexedDB):**
    1. With archiving on, open the app with no character selected: loaded characters that were never archived go back to the archive one per save. Record the time per character and check there is no out-of-memory.
    2. Open a character, edit it, move to another, and save: the edited one is archived again with the edit, and reopening it shows the edit.
    3. Open a character without editing, move on: it goes back to its archive file with no new write.
    4. Keep a group chat open: its members stay loaded.
    5. Start "Clean unused cold storage" while a re-archive is writing: nothing is lost, and the character is either archived or still loaded.
    6. Restart: the re-archived characters open with their content.
  - **Node server profile:** a character whose archive file would exceed the body limit stays loaded, shows no error, is not retried in the session and is not counted.
  - **Device pause:** on a device the boot pass has paused, no re-archive runs and edited characters are not held.
  - **Upstream round trip:** export a `.bin` after re-archiving and import it into upstream: re-archived characters open there; import an upstream `.bin` back.
- **Stage 2b:**
  - **Web (IndexedDB), one tab, on a profile with no pre-blocks copy or other older copy:** edit a character, move on, save; after the numbered automatic backups present at that save have rotated out, the old archive file is gone and the character still opens with the edit. Restart and load an older automatic backup: every character it names opens.
  - **Converted profile:** old archive files stay until "Clean unused cold storage" removes the pre-blocks copy (`MC-308` 2).
  - **Two tabs:** with a second tab open, or while the second tab loads a backup or runs the manual cleanup, no old archive file is deleted.
  - **Note 9 (Android, Tauri) and Windows (Tauri desktop):** the same single-tab check; the single instance holds (a second launch focuses the running window).
  - **Node server profile:** no old archive file is ever deleted.
- **Item 5, the idle reload removed:**
  - **Web (IndexedDB):** restore archived characters (well over 50 MiB), then leave the page idle for several minutes: it does not reload. Composer drafts behave as before within the page.
  - **Web, installed as an app:** open a card file with the app: it is imported once.
  - **Note 9 (Android, Tauri) and Windows (Tauri desktop):** start-up, the boot archive pass and re-archiving behave as before (no change expected; the desktop path was never on).
